c) if Upper Heyford airbase (which is in that direction) is ever used for military activity (I believe it was is
supposed to have been closed)

how the majority of sightings are explained/resolved (if indeed they are?). Plus historica data e.g. are
sightings becoming less or more common?

November. according to Thie

the base around the same the.
it said.
In the USA. highly supersonic
aircraft believed to be operating
from Nevada have been detected
and tracked by seismological
sensors installed by the US
Geological Service (USGS). The
booms were first recorded in
June last year.
Machrihanish is one of the
mosi remote bases in Europe.
located near the tip of the

Scotland. Recent base modern-

a

Meanwhile. the California
booms are the first substantial.
corroborated evidence ot
unidentified supersonic aircratt
operating over the US.A.
On at least four occasions.
sonic booms have registered on
some of the 220 sensors across
Souhem California. from the

edge ot the Mojave deser
according to Jim Mori. a U'SGS

maidents were recorded in June.
October. November and late

estimate that the target were
tying at speeds berween Mach
3 nd Mach 4.

headed north nd east over the

directly to southern Nevada.
500 km away. Most secret US
Los angeles at such speeds, the
largels were presumably
decelerating as they crossed the

its seismographs could detect

registered space shuule landings

ere characteristic of a smaller

JANES DEFENCE WEEKLY
2nd MAY 1992
Evidence grows of
Mach 3-4 aircraft
Souther California was rattled
by the latest in a series of
unexplained sonic booms on
16 April. The shock was felt
most around the San Gabriel
mountains, to the north of the
San Femando Valley.
This is the latest in a series of
booms occurring since June last
year. They have been linked to
highly
supersonic aircraft
developed under classified. or
'black', programmes (see
Briefing, p763).
In earlier incidents. a seis-
mograph net operated by US.
Geological Survey (USGS)
researchers at the California
USAF
Institute of Technology indica-
ted that the unidentified air-
craft were travelling at speeds
of Mach 3-4 in the direction of
USAF's secret Groom Lake
flight-test facility in Nevada
(JDW 29 Febuary).
The 16 April boom could
not be tracked as accurately
as some of the earlier events,
said USGS researcher Jim
Mori. but had the same charac-
teristics. Most booms have been
reported on Thursdays between
0630 and 0700 local time.

UNCLASSIFIED
SECRET
BACKGROUND NOTE - PQS 5983F and 5979F
These two questions from Martin Redmond MP and a third
probably been prompted by a recent article in the Scotsman
speculating that "America's most secret aircraft has been
operating in Scottish airspace" near RAF Machrihanish. The
aircraft is alleged to be a "very high supersonic, possibly
hypersonic" aircraft code-named "Aurora", and to be the "ultra-
secret replacement" for the SR-71 Blackbird reconnaissance
aircraft. Copies of the Scotsman article and a follow-up article.
in Jane's Defence Weekly are attached at Annex A. A copy of a
minute sent to APS/Secretary of State in reply to a request for
advice on the former article is attached at Annex B.
2.
The last SR-71 left the UK from RAF Mildenhall in January
1990 - this is public knowledge - and HQ 3rd Air Force advise
landed at RAF Machrihanish in the past year.
There may or may not be an Aurora project. There is no
knowledge in MOD of a "black" programme of this nature, although
what assistance the MOD has given to the US project) would
therefore be "none" - but this pre-supposes the existence of the
project. Nor would we be likely to be prepared to say publicly if
any MOD assistance had been given to such a project. To answer
instead that we have no knowledge of any such project is also
arguably inappropriate, since in circumstances where we were
aware we would probably not be prepared to say so. It is
therefore suggested that the question is answered by saying that
the existence of any such project (or operation) would be a
avoid any implication that the project exists.

UNCLASSIFLERET
BACKGROUND NOTE - PQs 5983F and 5979F
These two questions from Martin Redmond MP and a third
(due for answer later this week) from George Foulkes MP have
probably been prompted by a recent article in the Scotsman
speculating that "America's most secret aircraft has been
operating in Scottish airspace" near RAF Machrihanish. The
aircraft is alleged to be a "very high supersonic, possibly
hypersonic" aircraft code-named "Aurora", and to be the "ultra-
secret replacement" for the SR-71 Blackbird reconnaissance
aircraft.
Copies of the Scotsman article and a follow-up article
in Jane's Defence Weekly are attached at Annex A. A copy of a
minute sent to APS/Secretary of State in reply to a request for
advice on the former article is attached at Annex B.
7900 -
The last SR-71 left the UK from RAF Mildenhall in January
1990 - this is public knowledge - and HQ 3rd Air Force advise
has operated in Scottish airspace. The draft answer to PQ 5979F
demonstrates that no unusual aircraft (apart from an Antonov)
landed at RAF Machrihanish in the past year.
There may or may not be an Aurora project. There is no
knowledge in MOD of a "black" programme of this nature, although
and DIS if it did exist. The simple answer to PQ 5983F (about
what assistance the MOD has given to the US project) would
project. Nor would we be likely to be prepared to say publicly if
any MOD assistance had been given to such a project. To answer
instead that we have no knowledge of any such project is also
arguably inappropriate, since in circumstances where we were
aware we would probably not be prepared to say so. It is
therefore suggested that the question is answered by saying that
the existence of any such project (or operation) would be a
matter for the US authorities - "would be" rather than "is" to
avoid any implication that the project exists.

HEADLINE NEWS
rench firm's
new missile
SINGAPORE
Aerospatiale was due to unveil
' its MM 15 anti-ship missile at
the Asian Aerospace exhibition
in Singapore which opened on
25 February.
The ship-launched missile has
been developed in the last three
months, according to officials
from Aerospatiale's tactical
missiles division (Engins
Tactiques).
The MM 15, derived from the
AS 15 TT air-launched weapon
system, is designed to meet the
growing demand for con-
ventional lightweight weapons
to destroy small surface targets
within the 8-15 km range.
ẠỊOur new MM 15 missile
will be offered as a direct
competitor to the British
Aerospace Dynamics ship-
launched Sea Skua." a company
official said.
According to Aerospatiale.
the MM 15 could fill the gap
between the shorter range (8 km)
light naval artillery and long
range tactical anti-ship missiles
such as Exocet (20 km).
ẠIn the 8-15 km gap, there is
no effective conventional
method of dealing with small
targets which do not justity the
use of ExocetẠại
MM 15 has a maximum
effective range of 15 km. a
maximum speed of Mach 0.9
and weighs 103 kg with a 30 kg
warhead, says Aerospatiale. The
light weight allows operation by
small, fast surface vessels.
The MM 15 has no homing
head but is guided by a radar-
beam command-guidance tech-
nique via the Thomson-CSF
AGRION 15 (X-band, pulse-
compression) guidance radar
installed on the platform.
The radar is used with the
AS 15 TT heliborne missile
system in service with the
French, Saudi Arabian and
Bahraini navies. It tracks both
target and missile and computes
the error in range and bearing
between them.

may be Aurora

secretly developed and deployed
a hypersonic reconnaissance

UNCLE FIED
BACKGROUND NOTE - PQs 5983F and 5979F
These two questions from Martin Redmond MP and a third
speculating that "America's most secret aircraft has been
operating in Scottish airspace" near RAF Machrihanish. The
aircraft is alleged to be a "very high supersonic, possibly
hypersonic" aircraft code-named "Aurora", and to be the "ultra-
secret replacement" for the SR-71 Blackbird reconnaissance
aircraft. Copies of the Scotsman article and a follow-up article
in Jane's Defence Weekly are attached at Annex A. A copy of a
minute sent to APS/Secretary of State in reply to a request for
advice on the former article is attached at Annex B.
2.
The last SR-71 left the UK from RAF Mildenhall in January
1990 - this is public knowledge - and HQ 3rd Air Force advise
US Embassy have also confirmed that no secret new US aircraft
has operated in Scottish airspace. The draft answer to PQ 5979F
demonstrates that no unusual aircraft (apart from an Antonov)
landed at RAF Machrihanish in the past year.
There may or may not be an Aurora project. There is no
knowledge in MOD of a "black" programme of this nature, although
and DIS if it did exist. The simple answer to PQ 5983F (about
what assistance the MOD has given to the US project) would
therefore be "none" - but this pre-supposes the existence of the
project. Nor would we be likely to be prepared to say publicly if
any MOD assistance had been given to such a project. To answer
instead that we have no knowledge of any such project is also
arguably inappropriate, since in circumstances where we were
aware we would probably not be prepared to say so. It is
therefore suggested that the question is answered by saying that
the existence of any süch project (or operation) would be a
matter for the US authorities - "would be" rather than "is" to
avoid any implication that the project exists.
UNCLASSIFIED
SECRET

HEADLINE NEWS ETE
French firm's
new missile
SINGAPORE
Aerospatiale was due to unveil
its MM 15 anti-ship missile at
the Asian Aerospace exhibition
in Singapore which opened on
25 February.
The ship-launched missile has
been developed in the last three
months, according to officials
trom Aerospatiale's tactical
missiles division (Engins
Tactiques).
The MM 15, derived from the
AS 15 TT air-launched weapon
system, is designed to meet the
growing demand for con-
ventional lightweight weapons
to destroy small surface targets
within the 8-15 km range.
ẠOur new MM 15 missile
will be offered as a direct
competitor to the British
Aerospace Dynamics ship-
launched Sea Skua." a company
official said.
According to Aerospatiale.
the MM 15 could fill the gap
between the shorter range (8 km)
light naval artillery and long
range tactical anti-ship missiles
such as Exocet (20 km).
ẠIn the 8-15 km gap, there is
no effective conventional
method of dealing with small
targets which do not justify the
use of Exocet."
MM 15 has a maximum
effective range of 15 km. a
maximum speed of Mach 0.9
and weighs 103 kg with a 30 kg
warhead, says Aerospatiale. The
light weight allows operation by
small, fast surface vessels.
The MM 15 has no homing
head but is guided by a radar-
beam command-guidance tech-
nique via the Thomson-CSF
AGRION 15 (X-band, pulse-
compression) guidance radar
installed on the platform.
The radar is used with the
AS 15 TT heliborne missile
system in service with the
French, Saudi Arabian and
Bahraini navies. It tracks both
target and missile and computes
the error in range and bearing
between them.
Mystery contact
may be Aurora
Mounting evidence suggests
that the US Government has
secretly developed and deployed
a hypersonic reconnaissance

HEADLINE NEWS BERE
French firm's
new missile
SINGAPORE
Aerospatiale was due to unveil
its MM 15 anti-ship missile at
the Asian Aerospace exhibition
in Singapore which opened on
25 February.
The ship-launched missile has
been developed in the last three
months, according to officials
from Aerospatiale's tactical
missiles division (Engins
Tectiques).
The MM 15, derived from the
AS 15 TT air-launched weapon
system, is designed to meet the
growing demand for con-
ventional lightweight weapons
to destroy small surface targets
within the 8-15 km range.
ẠỊOur new MM 15 missile
will be offered as a direct
competitor to the British
Aerospace Dynamics ship-
launched Sea Skua." a company
official said.
According to Aerospatiale.
the MM 15 could fill the gap
between the shorter range (8 km)
light naval artillery and long
range tactical anti-ship missiles
such as Exocet (20 km).
ỊiIn the 8-15 km gap. there is
no effective conventional
method of dealing with small
targets which do not justify the
use of ExocetiẠạ
MM 15 has a maximum
effective range of 15 km. a
maximum speed of Mach 0.9
and weighs 103 kg with a 30 kg
warhead, says Aerospatiale. The
light weight allows operation by
small, fast surface vesseis.
The MM 15 has no homing
head but is guided by a radar-
beam command-guidance tech-
nique via the Thomson-CSF
AGRION 15 (X-band, pulse-
compression) guidance radar
installed on the platform.
The radar is used with the
AS 15 TT heliborne missile
system in service with the
French, Saudi Arabian and
Bahraini navies. It tracks both
target and missile and computes
the error in range and bearing
between them.
Mystery contact
may be Aurora
Mounting evidence suggests
that the US Government has
secretly developed and deployed
a hypersonic reconnaissance
aircraft. probably as a replace-
ment for the SR-71.
A Royal Air Force air traffic
controller tracked a target
leaving the NATO-RAF base at
Machrihanish, Scotland, at an
estimated speed of Mach 3 last
November, according to The
Scorsman newspaper last week.
Another witness heard an
extremely loud jet noise nEr
the base around the same time.
it said.
In the USA. highly supersonic
aircraft believed to be operling
from Nevada have been detected
and tracked by seismological
sensors installed by the US
Geological Service (USCS). The
booms were first recorded in
June last year.
Machrihanish is one of the
most remote bases in Europe,
located near the tip of the
Kintyre peninsula in Western
Scotland. Recent base modern-
ization
and a rumoured
association with the F-117
RECONNAISSANCE
BY BILL SWEETMAN
MINNEAPOLIS
Stealth Fighter lend credence 10
the new reports.
Meanwhile. the California
booms are the first substantial.
corroborated evidence of
unidentified supersonic aircraft
operating over the USA.
On at least four occasions.
sonic booms have registered on
some of the 220 sensors across
Southern California. from the
Los Angeles bin to the easter
edge of the Mojave desert.
according to Jim Mori. a USGS
seismologist at the California
Institute of Technology. The
incidents were recorded in June.
October. November and late
January. The seismologists
estimate that the targets were
flying at speeds between Mach
3 and Mach 4.
So far, all the tracks have been
headed north and east over the
Los Angeles basin. pointing
directly to southern Nevda.
500 km away. Most secret US
Air Force activities, including
the large flight-test base at
Groom Lake, are within the
Nellis range in Nevada. Since
the range is only 8 min trom
Los Angeles at such speeds, the
targets
were presumably
decelerating as they crossed the
coast.
The USGS first noticed that
its seismographs could detect
sonic
booms when they
registered space shuttle landings
at Edwards AFB. California.
Mor says the wave-forms
detected in the latest incidents
are characteristic of a smaller
vehicle than the 37 m long
shuttle orbiter. Neither the
shuttle nor the single SR-71B
which NASA maintains in flight
status were operating on the days
the booms were detected.
Reports that USAF is
developing hypersonic aircratt
in undisclosed "black" pro-
grammes date back to the mid-
1980s. In early 1988, the New
York Times reported that a
Mach 6. stealthy reconnaissance
aircraft called Aurora was being
developed to replace the SR-71,
which was retired in early 1990.

incorporating ejector ramjet engine fuelled by liquid methane or
hydrogen. Stealth would be maintained through ceramic radar absorbent
material able to withstand the fierce temperatures encountered at
hypersonic speeds (Julian Cook)

Nevada and California have
reported hearing extremely loud
or 'pulsing' noises caused by
unidentified aircraft.

WATER CHARGES: ASSISTANCE WITH
to PAYMENT
20%
Lord Molloy asked Her Majesty's Government:
What means will be considered for helping
poorer families who have difficulty in meeting rising
water bills.
Lord Henley: In April, income-related benefits will
be uprated fully in line with inflation. In addition,
people on income support will no longer be required
to meet local taxes. Effectively, this means that income
support rates will be increased by £750 million above
inflation, worth up to £145 in a full year for a couple.
Families on income support who have water charge
debts bills will continue to have access to direct
payments from benefit.
BANKS: COMPLAINTS FROM SMALL
BUSINESSES
Lord Inchyra asked Her Majesty's Government:
How many complaints they have received from
small businesses about their banks and how many
of those complaints were about interest rates or
interest margins.
The Minister of State, Department of Transport
(The Earl of Caithness): Between Ist November 1992
and 1st February 1993 we have received some 750
complaints from small businesses about their banks,
of which around 320 were about interest rates.
WATER METERS: ACCURACY
Lord Molloy asked Her Majesty's Governmen!
•What is the level of accuracy of water meters and
whether they consider this satisfactory.
The Parliamentary
Under-Secretary.
of State,
Department of Trade and Industry (Baroness Denton of
Wakefield): The permissible error limits for a meter
used for measuring the consumption of cold potable
water are detailed in the British Standard 5728, Part 1,
Section 5
This standard allows a maximum error limit of 5
per cent. in excess or in deficiency of the quantity
delivered when there is a minimum flow of water and
a maximum error limit of 2 per cent. in excess or
deficiency of the quantity delivered when the flow rate
range is between the transitional minimum and the
maximum that the meter is designed to operate at.
The National Weights and Measures Laboratory is
content that the level of accuracy of cold water meters
is satisfactory

I have received a FOI request from a regular correspondent asking for copies of documents on the subject of
the Aurora, the alleged hypersonic US experimental aircraft. As background, I should tell you that in March
1993 there were a number of UFO sightings that could not be readily explained with the result that ACAS
became involved and it was decided to ask the US if they were operating anything hush-hush over our
airspace. The sightings were recently the subject of a TV documentary on Channel 5 featuring a now, ex-civil
The general request is for correspondence for the period 1992-93 (inclusive) between the MoD and the US
authorities on the subject of the Aurora and whether it was flying over the UK. Whilst we need to be helpful, I
believe that the exam question is really getting at whether the US were flying Aurora over our airspace and
therefore do not see a need to include any general correspondence on the subject of the Aurora as a whole.
As well as the general question, the applicant is requesting a copy of BDS/RAF/3003 dated 14 Dec 1992
which was apparently forwarded from BDS to London (possibly to ACAS) and passed on USAF concern
about a possible stealthy aircraft flying from UK airspace. I believe the US Lt Gen Sectionmay have got the
wrong end of the stick and thought that we were operating a stealthy aircraft ourselves! We don't have a copy
on file here.
The applicant (who I would categorise as a reputable researcher) anticipates the possibility that any
documents would be withheld under Section 27 (International Relations) but suggests that after 13 years this
defence would not be viable. I would anticipate that withholding any documentation would result in an appeal.

Unfortunately, I have been unable to locate this information. Having spoken to colleagues in the branch that
produced the report, I am informed that their files containing UFO reports for that period have been destroyed.
it is likely (although I cannot categorically confirm it) that those reports are a duplicate of information held by
this office. I have located some 60 reports that were either made by police officers, (including military and
airport police) or list police officers as witnesses. I you would like to see this information please let me know
If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of your
request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible and you are still
dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting the Director of Information
Exploitation, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail Info-XD@mod.uk). Please note that
any request for an internal review must be made within two calendar months of the date on which the attempt
If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the Information
Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act. Please note that the
Information Commissioner will not investigate the case until the internal review process has been completed.
Further details of the role and powers of the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's
website, http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.ukiại
Please accept my apologies for the length of time it ha taken to respond to your request and I am sorry I could
not be of more assistance.

Dear Section 40
in your e-mail of 9 November 2006, you asked for a review of the MoD response to your
Freedom of Information request reference number 17-10-2006-155737-002. The branch responsible for
Your original request asked whether the Ministry of Defence website held any information on a General
Gabrielle who visited RAF Woodbridge/Bentwaters following the Rendlesham Forest incident in 1980. I
confirmed that it did and directed you to the relevant link which contains the MoD records relating to the
incident. In so doing it is believed that your request was answered and therefore we can see no further need
However, now that you have clarified the matter, it is clear from your e-mail of 9 November 2006 that this
response does not meet your intended requirements. I shall therefore register your e-mail as a new Freedom
of Information request and pass it to the relevant section to deal with. They will respond to you in due course.
The question of whether the Commanding Officer of Bentwaters/Woodbridge was informed of the visit of
General Gabriel may be best dealt with by the US authorities, who would, of course, be responsible for
informing their own personnel about the impending visit of one of their own senior officers. Might I suggest
that in addition to requesting information from the Ministry of Defence, you also contact the US authorities.
If you are unhappy with this response, you may take your complaint to the Information Commissioner under
the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act. Further details of the role and powers of the

If you do not have access to the internet at home, most public libraries have facilities for public use. The MOD
makes no charge to view, download or print this information. As the document consists of some 465 pages it
would not be an efficient use of public funds to produce and post paper copies to every individual who would
like a personal copy and your request is therefore refused in accordance with Section 21 of the Freedom of
Information Act 2000 as the information is already reasonably accessible to you by other means.
I am sorry to send what will be a disappointing reply. If you are dissatisfied with our decision to refuse
information or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of this request, then you should contact
the undersigned in the first instance. Should you remain dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal
2HB. (e.mail: InfoXD¿mod.uk).
